1	\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\label{sec:avoidance_source}
2	
3	Fine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Avoidance source attribution on SafeLIBERO. SDF at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ adds no measurable CAR beyond LoRA-only ($p > 0.1$); $\lambda = 0.1$ shows a marginal signal ($p \approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test, $n{=}2$) that requires additional seeds. All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\,0.37\,m.}
8	\label{tab:avoidance_source}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & Seeds & TSR (\%) & CAR (\%) & EE Disp (m) \\
14	\midrule
15	Pi0.5 baseline & 3 & 74.7\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 26.8\,$\pm$\,1.6 & 0.367 \\
16	\midrule
17	LoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\,$\pm$\,3.2 & 54.83\,$\pm$\,2.25 & 0.391 \\
18	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\,$\pm$\,3.0 & 54.5\,$\pm$\,2.29 & 0.387 \\
19	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 56.33\,$\pm$\,1.61 & 0.389 \\
20	LoRA+SDF $\lambda{=}0.1$\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\,$\pm$\,1.4 & 58.25\,$\pm$\,0.35 & 0.394 \\
21	\bottomrule
22	\end{tabular}
23	\vspace{1pt}
24	{\footnotesize \textsuperscript{$\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}
25	\end{table}
26	
27	Table~\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\% $\to$ 54.83\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\%) is within 0.33\,pp of $\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\,=\,57.5\%\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\_pudding highest, bbq\_sauce lowest; Figure~\ref{fig:pertask_fingerprint}) and invariant to $\lambda$ across a 20$\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
28	
29	\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\label{sec:tsr_collapse}
30	Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$21--25\,pp (74.7\% $\to$ 49.7--54.0\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\,0.37\,m displacement and 0\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.
31	
32	\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\label{sec:lambda_high}
33	Increasing $\lambda$ by 20$\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\,=\,58.25\%\,$\pm$\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\%, $\sigma = 2.04$; two-tailed permutation $p \approx 0.071$)\footnote{One-tailed permutation $p = 0.036$; we report the conservative two-tailed value in the main text. A directional prior is justified because the SDF loss is designed to \emph{reduce} collisions.}. This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\%, within 0.3$\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K is preserved across all other conditions (Figure~\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\sim}$3.6\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
34	
35	\begin{figure}[t]
36	\centering
37	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}
38	\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\lambda$ ($\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}
39	\label{fig:lambda_car}
40	\end{figure}
```

```text
1	\section{Experimental Setup and Metrics}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	\subsection{Benchmark and Models}\label{sec:benchmark}
4	
5	We evaluate on SafeLIBERO~\cite{hu2025vlsa}, the \textit{object\_pudding} suite (Level~II): four tabletop pick-and-place tasks with collision hazards and per-timestep collision detection. Two VLA families are tested: \textbf{Pi0} (${\sim}$2.5B)~\cite{black2024pi0} with flow matching~\cite{lipman2023flow}, and \textbf{Pi0.5} with broader pre-training. Table~\ref{tab:configurations} lists all configurations. Each uses 50 episodes per task (200 total); Pi0.5 multi-seed runs use 3 seeds (600 episodes). We report Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR), the fraction of episodes that complete the task~\cite{hu2025vlsa,zhang2025safevla}.
6	
7	\begin{table}[t]
8	\centering
9	\caption{Training configurations. Trainable parameters exclude the frozen backbone.}
10	\label{tab:configurations}
11	\vspace{2pt}
12	\small
13	\begin{tabular}{llr}
14	\toprule
15	Configuration & Description & Trainable Params \\
16	\midrule
17	Pi0 Full-FT & Full fine-tune, flow-matching loss & ${\sim}$2.5B \\
18	Pi0 LoRA & LoRA adapters only & ${\sim}$33.5M \\
19	Pi0+A1 (SDF aux)\textsuperscript{$\dagger$} & LoRA + SDF decoder + EE extractor & ${\sim}$658K \\
20	Pi0+A3 (learned SDF) & LoRA + SDF decoder w/ gradient refinement & ${\sim}$34.2M \\
21	Pi0.5 (pre-trained) & No fine-tuning & 0 \\
22	Pi0.5 LoRA & LoRA fine-tune on Pi0.5 & ${\sim}$33.5M \\
23	Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) & LoRA + SDF aux on Pi0.5, $\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\}$ & ${\sim}$34.2M \\
24	\bottomrule
25	\end{tabular}
26	\vspace{2pt}
27	\raggedright\footnotesize\textsuperscript{$\dagger$}LoRA weights not loaded due to parameter-name mismatch; only auxiliary heads trained.
28	\end{table}
29	
30	\subsection{Displacement-Filtered CAR}\label{sec:dfcar}
31	
32	Standard CAR conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse. We define an episode as \emph{action-collapsed} when $d_{\max}(e) < \tau_d$ ($\tau_d = 0.1$\,mm) and exclude such episodes:
33	\begin{equation}\label{eq:car_f}
34	\text{dfCAR} = \frac{|\{e : \text{safe}(e) \;\land\; d_{\max}(e) \geq \tau_d\}|}{|\{e : d_{\max}(e) \geq \tau_d\}|}
35	\end{equation}
36	The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal, with collapsed episodes below 1\,$\mu$m and active episodes above 200\,mm; any $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm yields identical results (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_threshold}).\footnote{See Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety} for details on the displacement measurement protocol.}
37	
38	\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Training and Oracle Refinement}\label{sec:sdf_aux}
39	
40	We augment LoRA-adapted~\cite{hu2022lora} VLAs with an SDF decoder and an EE position extractor (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_training}, Table~\ref{tab:hyperparams}). The total loss is:
41	\begin{equation}\label{eq:total_loss}
42	\mathcal{L}_\text{total} = \mathcal{L}_\text{action} + \lambda \left( \mathcal{L}_\text{SDF} + \mathcal{L}_\text{EE} \right)
43	\end{equation}
44	where $\lambda \in \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading and caused action collapse (TSR\,=\,0\%; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}). To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\label{sec:oracle_setup}
45	
```

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse, where near-zero motor output produces zero collisions through inaction rather than avoidance.
3	On SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\% CAR while 99.6\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\,mm. The robot is frozen, not safe.
4	We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes. The bimodal displacement distribution makes dfCAR robust across a $[2, 200]$\,mm threshold range.
5	We further show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision fails to deliver collision avoidance. At inference time, ground-truth oracle refinement reduces CAR below unrefined baselines because the SDF gradient drives unmonitored arm links into obstacles. During training, collision-free demonstrations alone account for all measured avoidance gains (${\sim}$28 percentage points). SDF supervision adds no significant benefit at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$) and only a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ ($p \approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test).
6	Displacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation. EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with collision avoidance, nor expected to deliver it.
7	\end{abstract}
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}
2	
3	Collision avoidance is a prerequisite for deploying Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models in physical environments~\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation. Recent methods report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR) through SDF-augmented training~\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\cite{cofreeevla2026}. Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.
4	
5	\begin{figure}[t]
6	\centering
7	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}
8	\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\tau_d$; Pi0+A1's 91.5\% safety score drops to ${\approx}$\,0\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}
9	\label{fig:teaser}
10	\end{figure}
11	
12	It is not (Figure~\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\,mm; TSR is 0\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \emph{fake safety}: \emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.
13	
14	We present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.
15	
16	\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\% to ${\approx}$\,0\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\,=\,74.7\%, CAR\,=\,26.8\%, with every safe episode above 288\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\ref{sec:fake_safety}).
17	
18	\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\% vs.\ 82--83\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\ref{sec:ee_only}).
19	
20	\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 with collision-free demonstrations raises CAR by ${\sim}$28 points with zero frozen episodes. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit at low loss weights ($\lambda \leq 0.01$, $p > 0.1$) and only a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ ($p \approx 0.071$, $n = 2$). The per-task avoidance ranking is preserved across all conditions, indicating that the demonstration distribution accounts for all observed avoidance (Section~\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).
21	
22	Our contributions are:
23	\begin{itemize}
24	    \item \textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm.
25	    \item \textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.
26	    \item \textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.
27	    \item \textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\sim}$28\,pp); SDF supervision adds no significant benefit at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ and only a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ ($n = 2$).
28	\end{itemize}
```

2	\label{sec:appendix_displacement}

8	\label{fig:displacement}

11	\section{Displacement Threshold Sensitivity Analysis}
12	\label{sec:appendix_threshold}
13	
14	To validate the robustness of our displacement threshold $\tau_d$, we evaluate the frozen rate across four orders of magnitude. Table~\ref{tab:threshold_sensitivity} shows that the fake safety pattern persists across all reasonable threshold choices: A1-LoRA exhibits near-total action collapse regardless of threshold, while Pi0.5+A1 maintains 0\% frozen rate even at the most conservative setting.
15	
16	\begin{table}[h]
17	\centering
18	\caption{Frozen rate (\% of episodes with $d_\mathrm{max} < \tau_d$) across threshold values.}
19	\label{tab:threshold_sensitivity}

21	\toprule
22	$\tau_d$ & A1-LoRA (Pi0) & Pi0.5 Baseline & Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) \\
23	\midrule
24	0.001 mm (1\,$\mu$m) & 73.9 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\
25	0.1 mm  & 99.6 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\
26	1.0 mm  & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\
27	10 mm   & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\
28	\bottomrule
29	\end{tabular}
30	\end{table}
31	
32	The choice of $\tau_d = 0.1$\,mm represents a conservative threshold: displacement below this value is physically negligible in manipulation tasks where typical action magnitudes are 1--10\,mm per step. Even at $\tau_d = 1$\,$\mu$m, 73.9\% of A1-LoRA safe episodes are classified as frozen, while all Pi0.5 episodes remain above threshold at every level tested.
33	
34	Table~\ref{tab:dfcar_threshold} extends this analysis to dfCAR across fine-tuned Pi0.5 configurations. All eight model-seed combinations yield identical dfCAR for $\tau_d \in [2, 200]$\,mm. At $\tau_d = 300$\,mm, 0--2 episodes per seed fall below threshold (displacement 200--300\,mm), causing $\leq$0.5\,pp deviation. Our conclusions hold across the entire tested range.

88	\section{Per-Task CAR Breakdown}\label{sec:appendix_pertask_car}
89

Chocolate Pudding is consistently highest and BBQ Sauce consistently lowest across all conditions, including $\lambda = 0.1$. The intermediate tasks (Orange Juice and Milk) exchange rank between seeds but remain bounded by the extremes. This invariance indicates that avoidance patterns reflect the spatial statistics of collision-free demonstrations rather than geometric supervision.

147	\section{Per-Task Oracle Analysis}
148	\label{sec:appendix_pertask}
149	\label{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}
150	
151	Table~\ref{tab:oracle_pertask} shows per-task CAR for oracle refinement. On task~1 (\texttt{chocolate\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\texttt{orange\_juice}) from 96.0\% to 82.5\% and task~2 (\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\% to 77.5\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.
152	
153	The feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.

175	\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}
176	\label{sec:appendix_null}
177	
178	We construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\% ($\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\% ($\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\% ($\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\% and standard deviation 2.04\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\% falls within $0.8\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\% (2 seeds) yields a two-tailed permutation $p \approx 0.071$ (one-tailed $p = 0.036$); a $z$-test gives an approximate $p \approx 0.039$ ($z = 1.76$). This marginal departure is inconclusive at $n = 2$.
179	
180	The permutation test makes no distributional assumptions. Pooling the six null values $\{57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5\}$ and two $\lambda = 0.1$ values $\{58, 58.5\}$ into eight measurements, we enumerate all $\binom{8}{2} = 28$ two-element combinations and compute the fraction whose mean $\geq 58.25$. Only one combination ($\{58, 58.5\}$, mean $= 58.25$) meets this threshold, yielding an exact one-tailed permutation $p = 1/28 \approx 0.036$ (two-tailed $p = 2/28 \approx 0.071$, as one combination on the lower tail also reaches equal distance from the pooled mean). The consistency of the exact permutation and approximate $z$-test results confirms that the marginal signal is robust to distributional assumptions but remains inconclusive at $n = 2$.
181	
182	A Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.
183

The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.

This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\sigma \approx 7$\,pp per task for $p \approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.
211	
212	
213	\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}
214	\label{sec:appendix_oracle}
215	
216	All oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\times$ 40).
217	
218	\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.
219	
220	\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.
221	
222	\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).
223	
224	
225	\section{Training Details}
226	\label{sec:appendix_training}
227	
228	\paragraph{Architecture.}
229	We use LoRA~\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\to$ 256 $\to$ 3).
230	
231	\paragraph{Training schedule.}
232	Training proceeds in two phases:
233	\begin{itemize}
234	    \item \textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.
235	    \item \textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.
236	\end{itemize}
237	
238	\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}
239	\begin{table}[h]
240	\centering
241	\caption{Training hyperparameters.}
242	\label{tab:hyperparams}
243	\begin{tabular}{ll}
244	\toprule
245	Parameter & Value \\
246	\midrule
247	Base model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\sim$3.5B parameters) \\
248	Optimizer & AdamW \\
249	Learning rate & $2.5 \times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\
250	Warmup steps & 1000 \\
251	Batch size & 32 \\
252	Total steps & 30000 \\
253	$\lambda_\mathrm{sdf}$ & \{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\} \\
254	$\lambda_\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\
255	SDF query points per step & 64 \\
256	SDF clamp $\delta$ & 0.05 m \\
257	Eikonal weight & 0.1 \\
258	LoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\
259	LoRA rank (action) & 32 \\
260	Phase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\
261	\bottomrule
262	\end{tabular}
263	\end{table}
264	
265	\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}
266	Each episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\sim}$5\% to ${\sim}$52\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).
267	
268	\paragraph{Data.}
269	We use the SafeLIBERO object\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.
270	
271	\paragraph{Computational resources.}
272	All training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.
273

On task~1 (\\texttt{chocolate\\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\\texttt{orange\\_juice}) from 96.0\\% to 82.5\\% and task~2 (\\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\\% to 77.5\\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.\n\nThe feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) for oracle SDF refinement variants. EE-only refinement helps only on task~1 where the EE is the primary collision body; on other tasks it trades one collision site for another.}\n\\label{tab:oracle_pertask}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Task 0 & Task 1 & Task 2 & Task 3 & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline & 96.0 & 46.0 & 98.0 & 88.0 & 82.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive & 82.5 & 55.0 & 77.5 & 85.0 & 75.0 \\\\\nOracle-fixed & 85.0 & 50.0 & 77.5 & 80.0 & 73.1 \\\\\nOracle-feature & 90.0 & 47.5 & 82.5 & 87.5 & 76.9 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_null}\n\nWe construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\\% and standard deviation 2.04\\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields a two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$ (one-tailed $p = 0.036$); a $z$-test gives an approximate $p \\approx 0.039$ ($z = 1.76$). This marginal departure is inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nThe permutation test makes no distributional assumptions. Pooling the six null values $\\{57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5\\}$ and two $\\lambda = 0.1$ values $\\{58, 58.5\\}$ into eight measurements, we enumerate all $\\binom{8}{2} = 28$ two-element combinations and compute the fraction whose mean $\\geq 58.25$. Only one combination ($\\{58, 58.5\\}$, mean $= 58.25$) meets this threshold, yielding an exact one-tailed permutation $p = 1/28 \\approx 0.036$ (two-tailed $p = 2/28 \\approx 0.071$, as one combination on the lower tail also reaches equal distance from the pooled mean). The consistency of the exact permutation and approximate $z$-test results confirms that the marginal signal is robust to distributional assumptions but remains inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,2.04\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally (two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",
      "numLines": 273,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 273
    }
  }
}

1	\section{The Fake Safety Problem}\label{sec:fake_safety}
2	
3	Standard CAR assumes that collision-free episodes reflect deliberate avoidance. This assumption is systematically violated: in every Pi0 configuration tested, over 99\% of collision-free episodes are action-collapse artifacts (Table~\ref{tab:fake_safety}).\footnote{Pi0-family values report obstacle displacement from the original protocol; Pi0.5 reports EE displacement from the rotation-corrected protocol (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}). Sub-millimeter displacement indicates action collapse in both cases.} Applying dfCAR collapses all three to ${\approx}$\,0\%. Pi0+A1 achieves the \emph{highest} raw CAR (91.5\%) because more complete action collapse (from a parameter-name mismatch preventing LoRA loading; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}) yields higher fake safety. Pi0.5 validates the metric from the opposite direction: every safe episode exceeds 200\,mm displacement, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (26.8\%\,$\pm$\,1.6). The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_displacement}), with a five-order-of-magnitude gap that makes $\tau_d$ robust.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Fake safety decomposition on SafeLIBERO. dfCAR excludes episodes with displacement $< \tau_d = 0.1$\,mm.}
8	\label{tab:fake_safety}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & Ep. & CAR (\%) & TSR (\%) & Fake Safe (\%) & dfCAR (\%) \\
14	\midrule
15	Pi0 Full-FT  & 200 & 83.0 & 0.5 & 99.4 & $\approx$\,0 \\
16	Pi0 LoRA     & 200 & 82.0 & 0.0 & ${\sim}$99 & $\approx$\,0 \\
17	Pi0+A1 (SDF) & 800 & 91.5 & 0.0 & 99.6 & $\approx$\,0$^*$ \\
18	\midrule
19	Pi0.5 (3-seed)  & 600 & 26.8\,$\pm$\,1.6 & 74.7\,$\pm$\,1.5 & 0.0 & 26.8 \\
20	\bottomrule
21	\end{tabular}
22	\vspace{1pt}
23	{\footnotesize $^*$\,3/732 safe episodes above threshold; denominator too small for reliable rate.}
24	\end{table}
25	
26	Figure~\ref{fig:tsr_car_scatter} reveals spatial structure in the TSR-CAR plane.\label{sec:anticorrelation} Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR, near-zero TSR), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR, low CAR). The upper-right quadrant (high TSR \emph{and} CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior; the upper-left is accessible through action collapse alone. Under dfCAR, Pi0 configurations drop to the origin: the apparent safety-capability tradeoff was an artifact of action-collapse contamination.
27	
28	\begin{figure}[t]
29	\centering
30	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig_tsr_car_scatter.pdf}
31	\caption{TSR vs.\ CAR for all configurations. Pi0-family models cluster in the upper-left (high CAR from inaction), Pi0.5 in the lower-right (high TSR with collision exposure), fine-tuned Pi0.5 in the center. The upper-right quadrant (high TSR and CAR) represents genuinely safe behavior. Error bars: $\pm$1 std across seeds.}
32	\label{fig:tsr_car_scatter}
33	\end{figure}

1	\section{End-Effector-Only SDF Cannot Ensure Full-Body Safety}\label{sec:ee_only}
2	
3	Could a perfect SDF signal enable effective EE-only refinement? We replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry (\S\ref{sec:oracle_setup}). Table~\ref{tab:oracle} and Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_bar} show that all three oracle variants achieve \emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines: the best oracle (76.9\%) falls 5.1 points below LoRA (82.0\%). Refinement with perfect distance information produces more collisions, not fewer.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Oracle SDF refinement with ground-truth distance fields. All oracle variants achieve lower CAR than unrefined baselines. Per-task breakdown in Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}.}
8	\label{tab:oracle}
9	\vspace{2pt}
10	\small
11	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
12	\toprule
13	Method & CAR (\%) & Fake Safe (\%) & Refines/ep \\
14	\midrule
15	LoRA baseline (no refine) & 82.0 & 99.4 & -- \\
16	Full-FT baseline (no refine) & 83.0 & -- & -- \\
17	\midrule
18	Oracle-adaptive (GT, dyn.\ $\alpha$) & 75.0 & 98.3 & 14.2 \\
19	Oracle-fixed (GT, $\alpha{=}0.005$) & 73.1 & 98.3 & 14.8 \\
20	Oracle-feature (GT, feat.\ space) & 76.9 & 96.7 & 241.7 \\
21	\midrule
22	A3 learned SDF & 77.1 & -- & 0.0 \\
23	\bottomrule
24	\end{tabular}
25	\end{table}
26	
27	\begin{figure}[t]
28	\centering
29	\includegraphics[width=0.85\linewidth]{fig_oracle_bar.pdf}
30	\caption{CAR for oracle SDF refinement versus unrefined baselines. All GT oracle variants underperform baselines despite perfect distance information.}
31	\label{fig:oracle_bar}
32	\end{figure}
33	
34	Per-task analysis reveals the mechanism (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}). On the one task where the EE is the primary collision body, oracle refinement helps (+9 points). On the remaining three, the SDF gradient pushes the EE away from obstacles but rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes; the net effect is one collision traded for another. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps per episode). EE-only refinement is structurally counterproductive: joint-space coupling between EE displacement and arm-link rotation redistributes collisions to unmonitored links regardless of signal quality.

Per-task breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}.}\n\\label{tab:oracle}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & CAR (\\%) & Fake Safe (\\%) & Refines/ep \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline (no refine) & 82.0 & 99.4 & -- \\\\\nFull-FT baseline (no refine) & 83.0 & -- & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive (GT, dyn.\\ $\\alpha$) & 75.0 & 98.3 & 14.2 \\\\\nOracle-fixed (GT, $\\alpha{=}0.005$) & 73.1 & 98.3 & 14.8 \\\\\nOracle-feature (GT, feat.\\ space) & 76.9 & 96.7 & 241.7 \\\\\n\\midrule\nA3 learned SDF & 77.1 & -- & 0.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\linewidth]{fig_oracle_bar.pdf}\n\\caption{CAR for oracle SDF refinement versus unrefined baselines. All GT oracle variants underperform baselines despite perfect distance information.}\n\\label{fig:oracle_bar}\n\\end{figure}\n\nPer-task analysis reveals the mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}). On the one task where the EE is the primary collision body, oracle refinement helps (+9 points). On the remaining three, the SDF gradient pushes the EE away from obstacles but rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes; the net effect is one collision traded for another. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps per episode). EE-only refinement is structurally counterproductive: joint-space coupling between EE displacement and arm-link rotation redistributes collisions to unmonitored links regardless of signal quality.",

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Across 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\S\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\S\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).
4	
5	\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}
6	Two independent causes explain the null result. \textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\sim}$28\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\S\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\,pp below unrefined baselines (\S\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}
9	dfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.
10	
11	\paragraph{Limitations.}
12	Our evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\approx}$0\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$21--25\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}); reduced exposure alone does not explain the pattern.
13	
14	\paragraph{Future work.}
15	Three directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.
16	
17	\paragraph{Broader impact.}
18	Contaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.
19	
```

</details>

1	\section{The Fake Safety Problem}\label{sec:fake_safety}
2	
3	Standard CAR assumes that collision-free episodes reflect deliberate avoidance. This assumption is systematically violated: in every Pi0 configuration tested, over 99\% of collision-free episodes are action-collapse artifacts (Table~\ref{tab:fake_safety}).\footnote{Pi0-family values report obstacle displacement from the original protocol; Pi0.5 reports EE displacement from the rotation-corrected protocol (\S\ref{sec:dfcar}). Sub-millimeter displacement indicates action collapse in both cases.} Applying dfCAR collapses all three to ${\approx}$\,0\%. Pi0+A1 achieves the \emph{highest} raw CAR (91.5\%) because more complete action collapse (from a parameter-name mismatch preventing LoRA loading; Table~\ref{tab:configurations}) yields higher fake safety. Pi0.5 validates the metric from the opposite direction: every safe episode exceeds 200\,mm displacement, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (26.8\%\,$\pm$\,1.6). The displacement distribution is sharply bimodal (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_displacement}), with a five-order-of-magnitude gap that makes $\tau_d$ robust.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\caption{Fake safety decomposition on SafeLIBERO. dfCAR excludes episodes with displacement $< \tau_d = 0.1$\,mm.}

  "tool_use_result": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515/projects/diff_collision_sdf/docs/paper/claim3.tex",
    "oldString": "Fine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes.",
    "newString": "Fine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$26 percentage points with zero frozen episodes.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\\label{sec:avoidance_source}\n\nFine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Avoidance source attribution on SafeLIBERO. SDF at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ adds no measurable CAR beyond LoRA-only ($p > 0.1$); $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows a marginal signal ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test, $n{=}2$) that requires additional seeds. All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\\,0.37\\,m.}\n\\label{tab:avoidance_source}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Seeds & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & EE Disp (m) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 3 & 74.7\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 26.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.6 & 0.367 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\\,$\\pm$\\,3.2 & 54.83\\,$\\pm$\\,2.25 & 0.391 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\\,$\\pm$\\,3.0 & 54.5\\,$\\pm$\\,2.29 & 0.387 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 56.33\\,$\\pm$\\,1.61 & 0.389 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\\,$\\pm$\\,1.4 & 58.25\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 & 0.394 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\\%) is within 0.33\\,pp of $\\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\\,=\\,57.5\\%\\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest; Figure~\\ref{fig:pertask_fingerprint}) and invariant to $\\lambda$ across a 20$\\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\\label{sec:tsr_collapse}\nFine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp (74.7\\% $\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\,0.37\\,m displacement and 0\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.\n\n\\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\\label{sec:lambda_high}\nIncreasing $\\lambda$ by 20$\\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\\,=\\,58.25\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\\%, $\\sigma = 2.04$; two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$)\\footnote{One-tailed permutation $p = 0.036$; we report the conservative two-tailed value in the main text. A directional prior is justified because the SDF loss is designed to \\emph{reduce} collisions.}. This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\\%, within 0.3$\\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K is preserved across all other conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\\sim}$3.6\\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}\n\\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}\n\\label{fig:lambda_car}\n\\end{figure}",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\\label{sec:avoidance_source}\n\nFine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$26 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Avoidance source attribution on SafeLIBERO. SDF at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ adds no measurable CAR beyond LoRA-only ($p > 0.1$); $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows a marginal signal ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test, $n{=}2$) that requires additional seeds. All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\\,0.37\\,m.}\n\\label{tab:avoidance_source}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Seeds & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & EE Disp (m) \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 1 & 71.5 & 28.5 & 0.367 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 51.5\\,$\\pm$\\,0.0 & 54.83\\,$\\pm$\\,2.25 & 0.391 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\\,$\\pm$\\,3.0 & 54.5\\,$\\pm$\\,2.29 & 0.387 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\\,$\\pm$\\,1.5 & 56.33\\,$\\pm$\\,1.61 & 0.389 \\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\\,$\\pm$\\,1.4 & 58.25\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 & 0.394 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\\%) is within 0.33\\,pp of $\\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\\,=\\,57.5\\%\\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest; Figure~\\ref{fig:pertask_fingerprint}) and invariant to $\\lambda$ across a 20$\\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\\label{sec:tsr_collapse}\nFine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp (74.7\\% $\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\,0.37\\,m displacement and 0\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.\n\n\\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\\label{sec:lambda_high}\nIncreasing $\\lambda$ by 20$\\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\\,=\\,58.25\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\\%, $\\sigma = 2.04$; two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$)\\footnote{One-tailed permutation $p = 0.036$; we report the conservative two-tailed value in the main text. A directional prior is justified because the SDF loss is designed to \\emph{reduce} collisions.}. This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\\%, within 0.3$\\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K is preserved across all other conditions (Figure~\\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\\sim}$3.6\\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}\n\\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}\n\\label{fig:lambda_car}\n\\end{figure}",

{

  "parent_tool_use_id": null,

  "tool_use_result": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515/projects/diff_collision_sdf/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%",
    "newString": "TSR\\,=\\,71.5\\%, CAR\\,=\\,28.5\\%",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:intro}\n\nCollision avoidance is a prerequisite for deploying Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models in physical environments~\\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation. Recent methods report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR) through SDF-augmented training~\\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}. Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\\tau_d$; Pi0+A1's 91.5\\% safety score drops to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure}\n\nIt is not (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\\,mm; TSR is 0\\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n\n\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n\n\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n\n\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 with collision-free demonstrations raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 points with zero frozen episodes. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\leq 0.01$, $p > 0.1$) and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($p \\approx 0.071$, $n = 2$). The per-task avoidance ranking is preserved across all conditions, indicating that the demonstration distribution accounts for all observed avoidance (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); SDF supervision adds no significant benefit at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($n = 2$).\n\\end{itemize}",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 13,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 13,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " ",
          " We present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.",
          " ",
          "-\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).",
          "+\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,71.5\\%, CAR\\,=\\,28.5\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).",

The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).",

The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n\n\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,71.5\\%, CAR\\,=\\,28.5\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n\n\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n\n\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 with collision-free demonstrations raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 points with zero frozen episodes. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\leq 0.01$, $p > 0.1$) and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($p \\approx 0.071$, $n = 2$). The per-task avoidance ranking is preserved across all conditions, indicating that the demonstration distribution accounts for all observed avoidance (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); SDF supervision adds no significant benefit at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($n = 2$).\n\\end{itemize}",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:intro}\n\nCollision avoidance is a prerequisite for deploying Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models in physical environments~\\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation. Recent methods report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR) through SDF-augmented training~\\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}. Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\\tau_d$; Pi0+A1's 91.5\\% safety score drops to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure}\n\nIt is not (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\\,mm; TSR is 0\\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n\n\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,71.5\\%, CAR\\,=\\,28.5\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n\n\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n\n\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 with collision-free demonstrations raises CAR by ${\\sim}$26 points with zero frozen episodes. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\leq 0.01$, $p > 0.1$) and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($p \\approx 0.071$, $n = 2$). The per-task avoidance ranking is preserved across all conditions, indicating that the demonstration distribution accounts for all observed avoidance (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); SDF supervision adds no significant benefit at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($n = 2$).\n\\end{itemize}",

    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse, where near-zero motor output produces zero collisions through inaction rather than avoidance.\nOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\% CAR while 99.6\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\,mm. The robot is frozen, not safe.\nWe introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes. The bimodal displacement distribution makes dfCAR robust across a $[2, 200]$\\,mm threshold range.\nWe further show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision fails to deliver collision avoidance. At inference time, ground-truth oracle refinement reduces CAR below unrefined baselines because the SDF gradient drives unmonitored arm links into obstacles. During training, collision-free demonstrations alone account for all measured avoidance gains (${\\sim}$28 percentage points). SDF supervision adds no significant benefit at $\\lambda \\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$) and only a marginal signal at $\\lambda = 0.1$ ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test).\nDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation. EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with collision avoidance, nor expected to deliver it.\n\\end{abstract}",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\n\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\sim}$28\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\approx}$0\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\sim}$21--25\\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}); reduced exposure alone does not explain the pattern.\n\n\\paragraph{Future work.}\nThree directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.\n\n\\paragraph{Broader impact.}\nContaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.\n",

Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.",

Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}",
          " dfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot."
        ]
      }
