    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\S\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\n\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\sim}$26\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\approx}$0\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested.     "originalFile": "\\section{Displacement Distribution}\n\\label{sec:appendix_displacement}\n\n\\begin{figure}[h]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig2_ee_displacement_distribution}\n\\caption{Displacement distribution across evaluation episodes. Pi0-family models (blue) cluster below 1\\,$\\mu$m, while Pi0.5 episodes (orange) exceed 200\\,mm. The five-order-of-magnitude gap makes $\\tau_d$ robust to any value in $[2, 200]$\\,mm.}\n\\label{fig:displacement}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Displacement Threshold Sensitivity Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_threshold}\n\nTo validate the robustness of our displacement threshold $\\tau_d$, we evaluate the frozen rate across four orders of magnitude. Table~\\ref{tab:threshold_sensitivity} shows that the fake safety pattern persists across all reasonable threshold choices: A1-LoRA exhibits near-total action collapse regardless of threshold, while Pi0.5+A1 maintains 0\\% frozen rate even at the most conservative setting.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Frozen rate (\\% of episodes with $d_\\mathrm{max} < \\tau_d$) across threshold values.}\n\\label{tab:threshold_sensitivity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n$\\tau_d$ & A1-LoRA (Pi0) & Pi0.5 Baseline & Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.001 mm (1\\,$\\mu$m) & 73.9 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n0.1 mm  & 99.6 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n1.0 mm  & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n10 mm   & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe choice of $\\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm represents a conservative threshold: displacement below this value is physically negligible in manipulation tasks where typical action magnitudes are 1--10\\,mm per step. Even at $\\tau_d = 1$\\,$\\mu$m, 73.9\\% of A1-LoRA safe episodes are classified as frozen, while all Pi0.5 episodes remain above threshold at every level tested.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:dfcar_threshold} extends this analysis to dfCAR across fine-tuned Pi0.5 configurations. All eight model-seed combinations yield identical dfCAR for $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm. At $\\tau_d = 300$\\,mm, 0--2 episodes per seed fall below threshold (displacement 200--300\\,mm), causing $\\leq$0.5\\,pp deviation. Our conclusions hold across the entire tested range.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{dfCAR (\\%) across displacement thresholds for fine-tuned Pi0.5 configurations ($n = 200$ episodes per seed). Values are identical across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm; at $\\tau_d = 300$\\,mm, at most 2 episodes are excluded.}\n\\label{tab:dfcar_threshold}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\footnotesize\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{8}{c}{$\\tau_d$ (mm)} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-9}\nCondition & 2 & 5 & 10 & 20 & 50 & 100 & 200 & 300 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA s0            & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 \\\\\nLoRA s1            & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.1 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.3 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ s0  & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ s1  & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.1 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ s2  & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Per-Task TSR Breakdown}\\label{sec:appendix_tsr_pertask}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:per_task_tsr} decomposes TSR by task for the avoidance source attribution experiments (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). All tasks retain meaningful success rates after fine-tuning, with Chocolate Pudding most resilient and Orange Juice most affected.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task TSR (\\%) at 30K training steps. LoRA-only reports seed~0 (rotation-fixed re-evaluation); SDF conditions report 3-seed means.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_tsr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nTask & Pi0.5 Base & LoRA-only\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ \\\\\n & (3 seeds) & (seed 0) & (3 seeds) & (3 seeds) \\\\\n\\midrule\nOrange Juice      & 70.7 & 18.0 & 33.3 & 24.7 \\\\\nChoco.\\ Pudding   & 86.0 & 72.0 & 78.0 & 76.7 \\\\\nMilk              & 66.0 & 32.0 & 35.3 & 48.7 \\\\\nBBQ Sauce         & 76.0 & 62.0 & 69.3 & 65.3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean              & 74.7 & 46.0 & 54.0 & 53.8 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}Seeds 1 and 2 are excluded because their rotation protocol differs from seed 0, making per-task TSR non-comparable.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task CAR Breakdown}\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_car}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:per_task_car} decomposes CAR by task for all fine-tuned conditions, supporting the claim that per-task ordering is preserved across SDF loss weights (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). Chocolate Pudding is consistently highest and BBQ Sauce consistently lowest across all conditions, including $\\lambda = 0.1$. The intermediate tasks (Orange Juice and Milk) exchange rank between seeds but remain bounded by the extremes. This invariance indicates that avoidance patterns reflect the spatial statistics of collision-free demonstrations rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) at 30K steps. $\\lambda{=}0.005$ and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ report 3-seed means. Per-task ordering (CP highest, BBQ lowest) is preserved across all conditions.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_car}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & OJ & CP & Milk & BBQ & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA s0 & 68 & 74 & 66 & 20 & 57.0 \\\\\nLoRA s1 & 54 & 68 & 72 & 16 & 52.5 \\\\\nLoRA s2 & 56 & 76 & 62 & 26 & 55.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3-seed) & 54.0 & 72.7 & 71.3 & 20.0 & 54.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3-seed) & 57.3 & 72.7 & 72.0 & 23.3 & 56.3 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s0 & 60 & 74 & 68 & 30 & 58.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s1 & 66 & 72 & 70 & 26 & 58.5 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nFor the $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition, seed~1 records \\texttt{avg\\_max\\_obstacle\\_displacement} of 0.0553\\,m (per-task: OJ\\,=\\,0.033, CP\\,=\\,0.055, Milk\\,=\\,0.041, BBQ\\,=\\,0.093), comparable to other fine-tuned conditions and consistent with active manipulation rather than frozen behavior.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig_pertask_car_fingerprint}\n\\caption{Per-task CAR fingerprint across conditions. Each bar shows the 3-seed mean; dots indicate individual seeds. The task ordering (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest) is preserved across all $\\lambda$ values, confirming that avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision. LoRA-only: 3-seed mean.}\n\\label{fig:pertask_fingerprint}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Safe Success Rate Analysis}\\label{sec:appendix_ssr}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:ssr} reports the Safe Success Rate (SSR\\,=\\,TSR\\,$\\cap$\\,CAR), the fraction of episodes achieving both task completion and collision avoidance. The SSR/TSR ratio quantifies the proportion of successful episodes that are also collision-free, controlling for task-completion rate.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{SSR and SSR/TSR ratio across conditions. The ratio increase from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (fine-tuned) indicates that CAR gains reflect learned safer execution, not reduced task exposure.}\n\\label{tab:ssr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & SSR (\\%) & SSR/TSR \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 74.7 & 26.8 & 25.0\\textsuperscript{a} & 0.33 \\\\\nLoRA-only (3 seeds) & 49.7 & 54.83 & 28.3 & 0.57 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3 seeds) & 54.0 & 54.5 & 27.8 & 0.51 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3 seeds) & 53.8 & 56.33 & 30.2 & 0.56 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 48.0 & 58.25 & 26.5 & 0.55 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{a}Estimated; per-episode overlap unavailable for 3-seed rotation-corrected protocol. \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}SSR from seed~1 only; seed~0 per-episode data overwritten.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task Oracle Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:oracle_pertask} shows per-task CAR for oracle refinement. On task~1 (\\texttt{chocolate\\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\\texttt{orange\\_juice}) from 96.0\\% to 82.5\\% and task~2 (\\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\\% to 77.5\\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.\n\nThe feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) for oracle SDF refinement variants. EE-only refinement helps only on task~1 where the EE is the primary collision body; on other tasks it trades one collision site for another.}\n\\label{tab:oracle_pertask}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Task 0 & Task 1 & Task 2 & Task 3 & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline & 96.0 & 46.0 & 98.0 & 88.0 & 82.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive & 82.5 & 55.0 & 77.5 & 85.0 & 75.0 \\\\\nOracle-fixed & 85.0 & 50.0 & 77.5 & 80.0 & 73.1 \\\\\nOracle-feature & 90.0 & 47.5 & 82.5 & 87.5 & 76.9 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_null}\n\nWe construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\\% and standard deviation 2.04\\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields a two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$ (one-tailed $p = 0.036$); a $z$-test gives an approximate $p \\approx 0.039$ ($z = 1.76$). This marginal departure is inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nThe permutation test makes no distributional assumptions. Pooling the six null values $\\{57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5\\}$ and two $\\lambda = 0.1$ values $\\{58, 58.5\\}$ into eight measurements, we enumerate all $\\binom{8}{2} = 28$ two-element combinations and compute the fraction whose mean $\\geq 58.25$. Only one combination ($\\{58, 58.5\\}$, mean $= 58.25$) meets this threshold, yielding an exact one-tailed permutation $p = 1/28 \\approx 0.036$ (two-tailed $p = 2/28 \\approx 0.071$, as one combination on the lower tail also reaches equal distance from the pooled mean). The consistency of the exact permutation and approximate $z$-test results confirms that the marginal signal is robust to distributional assumptions but remains inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,2.04\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally (two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,2.04\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally (two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Displacement Distribution}\n\\label{sec:appendix_displacement}\n\n\\begin{figure}[h]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig2_ee_displacement_distribution}\n\\caption{Displacement distribution across evaluation episodes. Pi0-family models (blue) cluster below 1\\,$\\mu$m, while Pi0.5 episodes (orange) exceed 200\\,mm. The five-order-of-magnitude gap makes $\\tau_d$ robust to any value in $[2, 200]$\\,mm.}\n\\label{fig:displacement}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Displacement Threshold Sensitivity Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_threshold}\n\nTo validate the robustness of our displacement threshold $\\tau_d$, we evaluate the frozen rate across four orders of magnitude. Table~\\ref{tab:threshold_sensitivity} shows that the fake safety pattern persists across all reasonable threshold choices: A1-LoRA exhibits near-total action collapse regardless of threshold, while Pi0.5+A1 maintains 0\\% frozen rate even at the most conservative setting.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Frozen rate (\\% of episodes with $d_\\mathrm{max} < \\tau_d$) across threshold values.}\n\\label{tab:threshold_sensitivity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n$\\tau_d$ & A1-LoRA (Pi0) & Pi0.5 Baseline & Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.001 mm (1\\,$\\mu$m) & 73.9 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n0.1 mm  & 99.6 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n1.0 mm  & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n10 mm   & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe choice of $\\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm represents a conservative threshold: displacement below this value is physically negligible in manipulation tasks where typical action magnitudes are 1--10\\,mm per step. All tasks retain meaningful success rates after fine-tuning, with Chocolate Pudding most resilient and Orange Juice most affected.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task TSR (\\%) at 30K training steps. LoRA-only reports seed~0 (rotation-fixed re-evaluation); SDF conditions report 3-seed means.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_tsr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nTask & Pi0.5 Base & LoRA-only\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ \\\\\n & (3 seeds) & (seed 0) & (3 seeds) & (3 seeds) \\\\\n\\midrule\nOrange Juice      & 70.7 & 18.0 & 33.3 & 24.7 \\\\\nChoco.\\ Pudding   & 86.0 & 72.0 & 78.0 & 76.7 \\\\\nMilk              & 66.0 & 32.0 & 35.3 & 48.7 \\\\\nBBQ Sauce         & 76.0 & 62.0 & 69.3 & 65.3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean              & 74.7 & 46.0 & 54.0 & 53.8 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}Seeds 1 and 2 are excluded because their rotation protocol differs from seed 0, making per-task TSR non-comparable.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task CAR Breakdown}\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_car}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:per_task_car} decomposes CAR by task for all fine-tuned conditions, supporting the claim that per-task ordering is preserved across SDF loss weights (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). Chocolate Pudding is consistently highest and BBQ Sauce consistently lowest across all conditions, including $\\lambda = 0.1$. The intermediate tasks (Orange Juice and Milk) exchange rank between seeds but remain bounded by the extremes. This invariance indicates that avoidance patterns reflect the spatial statistics of collision-free demonstrations rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) at 30K steps. $\\lambda{=}0.005$ and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ report 3-seed means. Per-task ordering (CP highest, BBQ lowest) is preserved across all conditions.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_car}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & OJ & CP & Milk & BBQ & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA s0 & 68 & 74 & 66 & 20 & 57.0 \\\\\nLoRA s1 & 54 & 68 & 72 & 16 & 52.5 \\\\\nLoRA s2 & 56 & 76 & 62 & 26 & 55.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3-seed) & 54.0 & 72.7 & 71.3 & 20.0 & 54.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3-seed) & 57.3 & 72.7 & 72.0 & 23.3 & 56.3 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s0 & 60 & 74 & 68 & 30 & 58.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s1 & 66 & 72 & 70 & 26 & 58.5 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nFor the $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition, seed~1 records \\texttt{avg\\_max\\_obstacle\\_displacement} of 0.0553\\,m (per-task: OJ\\,=\\,0.033, CP\\,=\\,0.055, Milk\\,=\\,0.041, BBQ\\,=\\,0.093), comparable to other fine-tuned conditions and consistent with active manipulation rather than frozen behavior.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig_pertask_car_fingerprint}\n\\caption{Per-task CAR fingerprint across conditions. Each bar shows the 3-seed mean; dots indicate individual seeds. The task ordering (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest) is preserved across all $\\lambda$ values, confirming that avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision. LoRA-only: 3-seed mean.}\n\\label{fig:pertask_fingerprint}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Safe Success Rate Analysis}\\label{sec:appendix_ssr}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:ssr} reports the Safe Success Rate (SSR\\,=\\,TSR\\,$\\cap$\\,CAR), the fraction of episodes achieving both task completion and collision avoidance. The SSR/TSR ratio quantifies the proportion of successful episodes that are also collision-free, controlling for task-completion rate.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{SSR and SSR/TSR ratio across conditions. The ratio increase from 0.39 (baseline) to 0.51--0.56 (fine-tuned) indicates that CAR gains reflect learned safer execution, not reduced task exposure.}\n\\label{tab:ssr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & SSR (\\%) & SSR/TSR \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 71.5 & 28.5 & 28.0 & 0.39 \\\\\nLoRA-only (3 seeds) & 51.5 & 54.83 & 27.7 & 0.54 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3 seeds) & 54.0 & 54.5 & 27.8 & 0.51 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3 seeds) & 53.8 & 56.33 & 30.2 & 0.56 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 48.0 & 58.25 & 26.5 & 0.55 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{a}Estimated; per-episode overlap unavailable for 3-seed rotation-corrected protocol. \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}SSR from seed~1 only; seed~0 per-episode data overwritten.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task Oracle Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:oracle_pertask} shows per-task CAR for oracle refinement. On task~1 (\\texttt{chocolate\\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\\texttt{orange\\_juice}) from 96.0\\% to 82.5\\% and task~2 (\\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\\% to 77.5\\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.\n\nThe feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) for oracle SDF refinement variants. EE-only refinement helps only on task~1 where the EE is the primary collision body; on other tasks it trades one collision site for another.}\n\\label{tab:oracle_pertask}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Task 0 & Task 1 & Task 2 & Task 3 & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline & 96.0 & 46.0 & 98.0 & 88.0 & 82.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive & 82.5 & 55.0 & 77.5 & 85.0 & 75.0 \\\\\nOracle-fixed & 85.0 & 50.0 & 77.5 & 80.0 & 73.1 \\\\\nOracle-feature & 90.0 & 47.5 & 82.5 & 87.5 & 76.9 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_null}\n\nWe construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\\% and standard deviation 2.04\\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields a two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$ (one-tailed $p = 0.036$); a $z$-test gives an approximate $p \\approx 0.039$ ($z = 1.76$). This marginal departure is inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nThe permutation test makes no distributional assumptions. Pooling the six null values $\\{57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5\\}$ and two $\\lambda = 0.1$ values $\\{58, 58.5\\}$ into eight measurements, we enumerate all $\\binom{8}{2} = 28$ two-element combinations and compute the fraction whose mean $\\geq 58.25$. Only one combination ($\\{58, 58.5\\}$, mean $= 58.25$) meets this threshold, yielding an exact one-tailed permutation $p = 1/28 \\approx 0.036$ (two-tailed $p = 2/28 \\approx 0.071$, as one combination on the lower tail also reaches equal distance from the pooled mean). The consistency of the exact permutation and approximate $z$-test results confirms that the marginal signal is robust to distributional assumptions but remains inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,2.04\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally (two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experimental Setup and Metrics}\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Benchmark and Models}\\label{sec:benchmark}\n\nWe evaluate on SafeLIBERO~\\cite{hu2025vlsa}, the \\textit{object\\_pudding} suite (Level~II): four tabletop pick-and-place tasks with collision hazards and per-timestep collision detection. Two VLA families are tested: \\textbf{Pi0} (${\\sim}$2.5B)~\\cite{black2024pi0} with flow matching~\\cite{lipman2023flow}, and \\textbf{Pi0.5} with broader pre-training. Table~\\ref{tab:configurations} lists all configurations. Each uses 50 episodes per task (200 total); Pi0.5 multi-seed runs use 3 seeds (600 episodes). We report Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR), the fraction of collision-free episodes, and Task Success Rate (TSR), the fraction of episodes that complete the task~\\cite{hu2025vlsa,zhang2025safevla}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training configurations. Trainable parameters exclude the frozen backbone.}\n\\label{tab:configurations}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llr}\n\\toprule\nConfiguration & Description & Trainable Params \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0 Full-FT & Full fine-tune, flow-matching loss & ${\\sim}$2.5B \\\\\nPi0 LoRA & LoRA adapters only & ${\\sim}$33.5M \\\\\nPi0+A1 (SDF aux)\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & LoRA + SDF decoder + EE extractor & ${\\sim}$658K \\\\\nPi0+A3 (learned SDF) & LoRA + SDF decoder w/ gradient refinement & ${\\sim}$34.2M \\\\\nPi0.5 (pre-trained) & No fine-tuning & 0 \\\\\nPi0.5 LoRA & LoRA fine-tune on Pi0.5 & ${\\sim}$33.5M \\\\\nPi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) & LoRA + SDF aux on Pi0.5, $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$ & ${\\sim}$34.2M \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\raggedright\\footnotesize\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}LoRA weights not loaded due to parameter-name mismatch; only auxiliary heads trained.\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Displacement-Filtered CAR}\\label{sec:dfcar}\n\nStandard CAR conflates genuine avoidance with action collapse. We define an episode as \\emph{action-collapsed} when $d_{\\max}(e) < \\tau_d$ ($\\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm) and exclude such episodes:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:car_f}\n\\text{dfCAR} = \\frac{|\\{e : \\text{safe}(e) \\;\\land\\; d_{\\max}(e) \\geq \\tau_d\\}|}{|\\{e : d_{\\max}(e) \\geq \\tau_d\\}|}\n\\end{equation}\nThe displacement distribution is sharply bimodal, with collapsed episodes below 1\\,$\\mu$m and active episodes above 200\\,mm; any $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm yields identical results (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_threshold}).\\footnote{See Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety} for details on the displacement measurement protocol.}\n\n\\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Training and Oracle Refinement}\\label{sec:sdf_aux}\n\nWe augment LoRA-adapted~\\cite{hu2022lora} VLAs with an SDF decoder and an EE position extractor (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_training}, Table~\\ref{tab:hyperparams}). The total loss is:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:total_loss}\n\\mathcal{L}_\\text{total} = \\mathcal{L}_\\text{action} + \\lambda \\left( \\mathcal{L}_\\text{SDF} + \\mathcal{L}_\\text{EE} \\right)\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\}$. In Pi0+A1, a parameter-name mismatch prevented LoRA loading and caused action collapse (TSR\\,=\\,0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:configurations}). To test the upper bound of EE-only refinement (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}), we also replace the learned SDF with ground-truth distance fields from the simulation geometry in three oracle variants (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_oracle}).\\label{sec:oracle_setup}\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Displacement Distribution}\n\\label{sec:appendix_displacement}\n\n\\begin{figure}[h]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig2_ee_displacement_distribution}\n\\caption{Displacement distribution across evaluation episodes. Pi0-family models (blue) cluster below 1\\,$\\mu$m, while Pi0.5 episodes (orange) exceed 200\\,mm. The five-order-of-magnitude gap makes $\\tau_d$ robust to any value in $[2, 200]$\\,mm.}\n\\label{fig:displacement}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Displacement Threshold Sensitivity Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_threshold}\n\nTo validate the robustness of our displacement threshold $\\tau_d$, we evaluate the frozen rate across four orders of magnitude. Table~\\ref{tab:threshold_sensitivity} shows that the fake safety pattern persists across all reasonable threshold choices: A1-LoRA exhibits near-total action collapse regardless of threshold, while Pi0.5+A1 maintains 0\\% frozen rate even at the most conservative setting.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Frozen rate (\\% of episodes with $d_\\mathrm{max} < \\tau_d$) across threshold values.}\n\\label{tab:threshold_sensitivity}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n$\\tau_d$ & A1-LoRA (Pi0) & Pi0.5 Baseline & Pi0.5+A1 (SDF aux) \\\\\n\\midrule\n0.001 mm (1\\,$\\mu$m) & 73.9 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n0.1 mm  & 99.6 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n1.0 mm  & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n10 mm   & 100.0 & 0.0 & 0.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe choice of $\\tau_d = 0.1$\\,mm represents a conservative threshold: displacement below this value is physically negligible in manipulation tasks where typical action magnitudes are 1--10\\,mm per step. Even at $\\tau_d = 1$\\,$\\mu$m, 73.9\\% of A1-LoRA safe episodes are classified as frozen, while all Pi0.5 episodes remain above threshold at every level tested.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:dfcar_threshold} extends this analysis to dfCAR across fine-tuned Pi0.5 configurations. All eight model-seed combinations yield identical dfCAR for $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm. At $\\tau_d = 300$\\,mm, 0--2 episodes per seed fall below threshold (displacement 200--300\\,mm), causing $\\leq$0.5\\,pp deviation. Our conclusions hold across the entire tested range.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{dfCAR (\\%) across displacement thresholds for fine-tuned Pi0.5 configurations ($n = 200$ episodes per seed). Values are identical across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm; at $\\tau_d = 300$\\,mm, at most 2 episodes are excluded.}\n\\label{tab:dfcar_threshold}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\footnotesize\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{8}{c}{$\\tau_d$ (mm)} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-9}\nCondition & 2 & 5 & 10 & 20 & 50 & 100 & 200 & 300 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA s0            & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 \\\\\nLoRA s1            & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.1 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 & 54.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.5 & 52.3 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ s0  & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 & 57.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ s1  & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.5 & 57.1 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ s2  & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.5 & 54.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Per-Task TSR Breakdown}\\label{sec:appendix_tsr_pertask}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:per_task_tsr} decomposes TSR by task for the avoidance source attribution experiments (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). All tasks retain meaningful success rates after fine-tuning, with Chocolate Pudding most resilient and Orange Juice most affected.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task TSR (\\%) at 30K training steps. LoRA-only reports seed~0 (rotation-fixed re-evaluation); SDF conditions report 3-seed means.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_tsr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nTask & Pi0.5 Base & LoRA-only\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & SDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ & SDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ \\\\\n & (3 seeds) & (seed 0) & (3 seeds) & (3 seeds) \\\\\n\\midrule\nOrange Juice      & 70.7 & 18.0 & 33.3 & 24.7 \\\\\nChoco.\\ Pudding   & 86.0 & 72.0 & 78.0 & 76.7 \\\\\nMilk              & 66.0 & 32.0 & 35.3 & 48.7 \\\\\nBBQ Sauce         & 76.0 & 62.0 & 69.3 & 65.3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean              & 74.7 & 46.0 & 54.0 & 53.8 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}Seeds 1 and 2 are excluded because their rotation protocol differs from seed 0, making per-task TSR non-comparable.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task CAR Breakdown}\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_car}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:per_task_car} decomposes CAR by task for all fine-tuned conditions, supporting the claim that per-task ordering is preserved across SDF loss weights (\\S\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). Chocolate Pudding is consistently highest and BBQ Sauce consistently lowest across all conditions, including $\\lambda = 0.1$. The intermediate tasks (Orange Juice and Milk) exchange rank between seeds but remain bounded by the extremes. This invariance indicates that avoidance patterns reflect the spatial statistics of collision-free demonstrations rather than geometric supervision.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) at 30K steps. $\\lambda{=}0.005$ and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ report 3-seed means. Per-task ordering (CP highest, BBQ lowest) is preserved across all conditions.}\n\\label{tab:per_task_car}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & OJ & CP & Milk & BBQ & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA s0 & 68 & 74 & 66 & 20 & 57.0 \\\\\nLoRA s1 & 54 & 68 & 72 & 16 & 52.5 \\\\\nLoRA s2 & 56 & 76 & 62 & 26 & 55.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3-seed) & 54.0 & 72.7 & 71.3 & 20.0 & 54.5 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3-seed) & 57.3 & 72.7 & 72.0 & 23.3 & 56.3 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s0 & 60 & 74 & 68 & 30 & 58.0 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$ s1 & 66 & 72 & 70 & 26 & 58.5 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nFor the $\\lambda = 0.1$ condition, seed~1 records \\texttt{avg\\_max\\_obstacle\\_displacement} of 0.0553\\,m (per-task: OJ\\,=\\,0.033, CP\\,=\\,0.055, Milk\\,=\\,0.041, BBQ\\,=\\,0.093), comparable to other fine-tuned conditions and consistent with active manipulation rather than frozen behavior.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig_pertask_car_fingerprint}\n\\caption{Per-task CAR fingerprint across conditions. Each bar shows the 3-seed mean; dots indicate individual seeds. The task ordering (chocolate\\_pudding highest, bbq\\_sauce lowest) is preserved across all $\\lambda$ values, confirming that avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision. LoRA-only: 3-seed mean.}\n\\label{fig:pertask_fingerprint}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\section{Safe Success Rate Analysis}\\label{sec:appendix_ssr}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:ssr} reports the Safe Success Rate (SSR\\,=\\,TSR\\,$\\cap$\\,CAR), the fraction of episodes achieving both task completion and collision avoidance. The SSR/TSR ratio quantifies the proportion of successful episodes that are also collision-free, controlling for task-completion rate.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{SSR and SSR/TSR ratio across conditions. The ratio increase from 0.39 (baseline) to 0.51--0.56 (fine-tuned) indicates that CAR gains reflect learned safer execution, not reduced task exposure.}\n\\label{tab:ssr}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nCondition & TSR (\\%) & CAR (\\%) & SSR (\\%) & SSR/TSR \\\\\n\\midrule\nPi0.5 baseline & 71.5 & 28.5 & 28.0 & 0.39 \\\\\nLoRA-only (3 seeds) & 51.5 & 54.83 & 27.7 & 0.54 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (3 seeds) & 54.0 & 54.5 & 27.8 & 0.51 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (3 seeds) & 53.8 & 56.33 & 30.2 & 0.56 \\\\\nSDF $\\lambda{=}0.1$\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & 48.0 & 58.25 & 26.5 & 0.55 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\footnotesize \\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$}SSR from seed~1 only; seed~0 per-episode data overwritten.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Per-Task Oracle Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask}\n\\label{sec:appendix_pertask_oracle}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:oracle_pertask} shows per-task CAR for oracle refinement. On task~1 (\\texttt{chocolate\\_pudding}), where the EE is the primary collision body and the LoRA baseline achieves only 46.0\\% CAR, Oracle-adaptive improves to 55.0\\% and Oracle-feature to 47.5\\%. On the remaining tasks, all oracle variants degrade: Oracle-adaptive reduces task~0 (\\texttt{orange\\_juice}) from 96.0\\% to 82.5\\% and task~2 (\\texttt{milk}) from 98.0\\% to 77.5\\%. The SDF gradient pushes the EE away from the nearest surface, but the resulting joint-space displacement rotates intermediate arm links into obstacle volumes.\n\nThe feature-space oracle fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements/episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49), and CAR (76.9\\%) shows no advantage despite 17$\\times$ more steps. The A3 learned SDF never triggers refinement (0 steps/episode), as its outputs avoid the activation threshold.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-task CAR (\\%) for oracle SDF refinement variants. EE-only refinement helps only on task~1 where the EE is the primary collision body; on other tasks it trades one collision site for another.}\n\\label{tab:oracle_pertask}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Task 0 & Task 1 & Task 2 & Task 3 & Agg. \\\\\n\\midrule\nLoRA baseline & 96.0 & 46.0 & 98.0 & 88.0 & 82.0 \\\\\n\\midrule\nOracle-adaptive & 82.5 & 55.0 & 77.5 & 85.0 & 75.0 \\\\\nOracle-fixed & 85.0 & 50.0 & 77.5 & 80.0 & 73.1 \\\\\nOracle-feature & 90.0 & 47.5 & 82.5 & 87.5 & 76.9 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\section{Null Distribution for SDF Loss Weight Analysis}\n\\label{sec:appendix_null}\n\nWe construct a null distribution by pooling the three LoRA-only seeds and three $\\lambda = 0.005$ seeds into six per-seed CAR measurements: 57.0\\% (LoRA s0, re-evaluated), 52.5\\% (LoRA s1), 55.0\\% (LoRA s2), 57.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s0), 54.0\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s1), 52.5\\% ($\\lambda{=}0.005$ s2), yielding mean 54.67\\% and standard deviation 2.04\\% (Bessel-corrected, $n{-}1 = 5$). We treat these as exchangeable because SDF at $\\lambda = 0.005$ produces no separation from LoRA-only. The $\\lambda = 0.01$ mean of 56.33\\% falls within $0.8\\sigma$ of this null ($p > 0.1$), consistent with seed-to-seed variation rather than a treatment effect. The $\\lambda = 0.1$ 30K mean of 58.25\\% (2 seeds) yields a two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$ (one-tailed $p = 0.036$); a $z$-test gives an approximate $p \\approx 0.039$ ($z = 1.76$). This marginal departure is inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nThe permutation test makes no distributional assumptions. Pooling the six null values $\\{57, 52.5, 55, 57, 54, 52.5\\}$ and two $\\lambda = 0.1$ values $\\{58, 58.5\\}$ into eight measurements, we enumerate all $\\binom{8}{2} = 28$ two-element combinations and compute the fraction whose mean $\\geq 58.25$. Only one combination ($\\{58, 58.5\\}$, mean $= 58.25$) meets this threshold, yielding an exact one-tailed permutation $p = 1/28 \\approx 0.036$ (two-tailed $p = 2/28 \\approx 0.071$, as one combination on the lower tail also reaches equal distance from the pooled mean). The consistency of the exact permutation and approximate $z$-test results confirms that the marginal signal is robust to distributional assumptions but remains inconclusive at $n = 2$.\n\nA Phase~2 ablation reinforces this conclusion. Setting $\\lambda = 0$ during Phase~2 (steps 5K--30K), so that SDF supervision is active only during auxiliary head warmup (Phase~1, steps 0--5K), yields CAR of 57.5\\% (single seed, s2). This value lies within the null range, indicating that SDF training produces no lasting behavioral effect on collision avoidance.\n\n\\section{Step-wise Checkpoint Analysis for \\texorpdfstring{$\\lambda = 0.1$}{lambda = 0.1}}\n\\label{sec:appendix_stepwise}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:stepwise_lambda01} reports per-seed CAR at seven checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$. Seed-1 data is available at 16K, 20K, 22K, and 30K; 24K--28K report seed-0 only. The seed-0 curve shows no monotonic trend through 28K; the 30K checkpoint marks the first consistent dual-seed departure from the null band, though whether this reflects a genuine late-training effect or sampling variance at $n = 2$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed CAR (\\%) at training checkpoints for $\\lambda = 0.1$ (200 episodes each). 24K--28K: seed-0 only. Dual-seed means remain within the null band (54.67\\%\\,$\\pm$\\,2.04\\%) at all checkpoints except 30K, where both seeds depart marginally (two-tailed permutation $p \\approx 0.071$).}\n\\label{tab:stepwise_lambda01}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nCheckpoint & Seed 0 & Seed 1 & Mean \\\\\n\\midrule\n16K steps & 55.5 & 56.0 & 55.75 \\\\\n20K steps & 55.0 & 56.0 & 55.5 \\\\\n22K steps & 60.5 & 56.5 & 58.5 \\\\\n24K steps & 52.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n26K steps & 53.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n28K steps & 54.5 & -- & -- \\\\\n30K steps & 58.0 & 58.5 & 58.25 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nIndividual seeds exhibit checkpoint-to-checkpoint CAR fluctuations of up to 8\\,pp (seed~0: 52.5--60.5\\%), larger than the cross-seed standard deviation at any fixed checkpoint ($\\leq$2.8\\,pp). This is consistent with binomial sampling noise at $n = 50$ episodes per task ($\\sigma \\approx 7$\\,pp per task for $p \\approx 0.5$). The seed-averaged curve shows no monotonic trend, confirming that SDF loss weight does not produce a progressive training signal.\n\n\n\\section{Oracle SDF Refinement Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_oracle}\n\nAll oracle variants replace the learned SDF decoder with ground-truth distance fields computed from the simulation geometry. Each is evaluated for 160 episodes (4 tasks $\\times$ 40).\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-adaptive.} At each timestep, if the ground-truth SDF at the end-effector position falls below a safety margin, the action is adjusted by a step in the negative SDF gradient direction. The step size $\\alpha$ is scaled inversely with distance: smaller distances produce larger corrections. This mimics an adaptive potential field controller.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-fixed.} Identical to Oracle-adaptive except the step size is fixed at $\\alpha = 0.005$ regardless of distance. This isolates the effect of step-size adaptation from the refinement geometry.\n\n\\paragraph{Oracle-feature.} Instead of modifying the action directly in Cartesian space, this variant applies the SDF gradient in the policy's latent feature space via backpropagation. The gradient of the ground-truth SDF value with respect to the intermediate features is computed and used to perturb the features before the action head decodes them. This tests whether geometric corrections are more effective when applied in the model's learned representation space. In practice, the feature-space variant fires on nearly every timestep (241.7 refinements per episode vs.\\ 14.2 for Cartesian variants) but with gradient magnitudes two orders of magnitude smaller (0.004--0.006 vs.\\ 0.18--0.49 in action space).\n\n\n\\section{Training Details}\n\\label{sec:appendix_training}\n\n\\paragraph{Architecture.}\nWe use LoRA~\\citep{hu2022lora} adapters injected into the PaliGemma vision encoder (rank 16, $\\alpha$=16) and the action expert (rank 32, $\\alpha$=32), following the configuration of OpenPi. The SDF decoder is a 4-layer MLP (hidden dimension 256) with skip connections, taking pooled suffix features (1024-d) and query points (3-d) as input. The end-effector extractor is a 2-layer MLP (1024 $\\to$ 256 $\\to$ 3).\n\n\\paragraph{Training schedule.}\nTraining proceeds in two phases:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 1} (steps 0--5000): Backbone and LoRA parameters are frozen. Only the SDF decoder and EE extractor are trained, allowing them to learn meaningful representations before the policy adapts.\n    \\item \\textbf{Phase 2} (steps 5000--30000): LoRA parameters are unfrozen with a 100-step gradient warmup ramp. All auxiliary heads continue training jointly with the policy.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\nParameter & Value \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase model & Pi0.5 (OpenPi, $\\sim$3.5B parameters) \\\\\nOptimizer & AdamW \\\\\nLearning rate & $2.5 \\times 10^{-5}$ (cosine decay to $\\mathrm{lr}/10$) \\\\\nWarmup steps & 1000 \\\\\nBatch size & 32 \\\\\nTotal steps & 30000 \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{sdf}$ & \\{0.005, 0.01, 0.1\\} \\\\\n$\\lambda_\\mathrm{ee}$ & 0.01 \\\\\nSDF query points per step & 64 \\\\\nSDF clamp $\\delta$ & 0.05 m \\\\\nEikonal weight & 0.1 \\\\\nLoRA rank (vision) & 16 \\\\\nLoRA rank (action) & 32 \\\\\nPhase 1 duration & 5000 steps \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nEach episode runs for at most 300 timesteps. The policy generates a 50-step action trajectory via flow matching and executes the first 5 steps before replanning. An episode terminates early upon task completion. Collision detection uses MuJoCo's built-in contact detection: any contact between the robot and a non-target object registers as a collision. For Pi0.5 evaluations, camera images are rotated 180$^\\circ$ to match the pre-training viewpoint convention; this correction was verified to restore TSR from ${\\sim}$5\\% to ${\\sim}$52\\% while leaving CAR comparable. Oracle variants use 40 episodes per task (160 total) with a single seed; all other configurations use 50 episodes per task (200 total).\n\n\\paragraph{Data.}\nWe use the SafeLIBERO object\\_pudding suite with pre-computed SDF ground truth. SDF values are computed offline from the obstacle mesh using a dense $64^3$ voxel grid. During training, 64 query points are randomly sampled per step from this grid.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational resources.}\nAll training and evaluation experiments are conducted on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs (96\\,GB). Phase~1 (5K steps) takes approximately 6 hours on a single GPU; Phase~2 (25K steps) takes approximately 32 hours. Evaluation of 200 episodes requires approximately 4 hours. Across 100+ experiments (training runs, evaluations, oracle variants, and ablations), the total computational budget is approximately 700 GPU-hours.\n",

27	Table~\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\sim}$26 percentage points (28.5\% $\to$ 54.83\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\%) is within 0.33\,pp of $\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\,=\,57.5\%\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\_pudding highest, bbq\_sauce lowest; Figure~\ref{fig:pertask_fingerprint}) and invariant to $\lambda$ across a 20$\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.
28	
29	\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\label{sec:tsr_collapse}
30	Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\sim}$18--20\,pp (71.5\% $\to$ 51.5--54.0\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\,0.37\,m displacement and 0\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.39 (baseline) to 0.51--0.56 (Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.
31	
32	\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\label{sec:lambda_high}
33	Increasing $\lambda$ by 20$\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\,=\,58.25\%\,$\pm$\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\%, $\sigma = 2.04$; two-tailed permutation $p \approx 0.071$)\footnote{One-tailed permutation $p = 0.036$; we report the conservative two-tailed value in the main text. A directional prior is justified because the SDF loss is designed to \emph{reduce} collisions.}. This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\%, within 0.3$\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K is preserved across all other conditions (Figure~\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\lambda \leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\sim}$3.6\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.

Low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}\n\\label{fig:lambda_car}\n\\end{figure}",
